1	# Notes
2	
3	This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
4	Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
5	
6	By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
7	about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
8	
9	In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
10	database to better manage the information, TBD.
11	
12	Examples chore file:
13	```
14	# Chores-01.md
15	 
16	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
17	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
18	how and why of a particular chore.
19	
20	## Create a binary that lists jj info 
21	
22	This binary should list the changeID, commitID, and description title
23	and using `jj-lib`
24	```
25	
26	## Workflow and conventions
27	
28	Bot-facing workflow, versioning, and code conventions live in
29	[`../CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md). Start there for:
30	
31	- **Versioning during development** — single-step vs multi-step,
32	  `-N` pre-release suffixes, done-marker discipline.
33	- **Code Conventions** — doc comments on every file / fn / method,
34	  `// OK: …` justifications on `unwrap*` calls, ask-on-ambiguity,
35	  stuck detection.
36	- **Commit-Push-Finalize Flow** — two-checkpoint per-step
37	  discipline with hard stop after finalize.
38	
39	## Todo format
40	
41	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
42	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
43	references.
44	
45	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
46	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
47	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
48	
49	Examples:
50	
51	# Todo
52	- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
53	- Fix bug Y [1]
54	
55	# Done
56	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
57	
58	[1]: bugs.md#bug-y
59	[2]: issues.md#issue-z
60	[3]: fixes.md#fix-z
61	
```

9	## Todo
10	
11	A markdown list of task to do in the near feature
12	
13	 - sync: surface working-copy state in the up-to-date summary (per-repo
14	   pending-files count or compact stat). Wording-only fix shipped in
15	   0.37.1; this is the design+impl of the actual signal. [54]
16	 - Add `status` (alias `st`) subcommand: `jj st` across both repos in
17	   one shot. Natural home for the working-copy signal called out in [54].
18	 - Mirror `--check` / `--no-check` onto `vc-x1 push` (forwards through to
19	   the preflight `vc-x1 sync` invocation). 0.37.1 hard-codes `--check`;
20	   `--no-check` would be the user-opt-in to "auto-rebase under the gates,
21	   I trust the sync state". Default stays `--check`.
22	 - `vc-x1 push`: `--message-file PATH` flag. Reads a git-style commit
23	   message file — first line is title, blank line, rest is body.
24	   Equivalent to today's `--title "$(cat t)" --body "$(cat b)"` but
25	   one flag, one file, no shell escaping gymnastics. Motivation
26	   (2026-04-22): when scripting a push with a long multi-line body,
27	   the `--title "$T" --body "$B"` pattern makes the actual `vc-x1
28	   push …` invocation visually disappear under the variable
29	   assignments in terminal output — hard to scan. A file-based
30	   message keeps the invocation short. No short form (`-m`, `-F`,
31	   etc.) — infrequent enough that the long name is fine. Default `both` matches
32	   current behavior. `--scope=app` or `--scope=claude` runs push against
33	   one repo only — for docs-only fixes on the app side, or a `.claude`
34	   finalize that doesn't need to bundle app changes. Independent of
35	   the squash work below; simpler and self-contained. **Warn on
36	   scope/WC mismatch**: if `--scope=app` is chosen and `.claude` has
37	   pending changes (or vice versa), emit a non-fatal warning
38	   naming the pending files — catches the "I meant --scope=both"
39	   case before the user commits.
40	 - `vc-x1 push`: `--squash` flag. Instead of creating a new commit,
41	   squashes WC into the current repo's `@-` via `--ignore-immutable`
42	   and force-pushes. Message stage pre-fills `$EDITOR` with the
43	   existing description so typical use is edit-in-place; `--title`
44	   / `--body` still override. Motivation: the manual squash +
45	   describe + force-push sequence (CLAUDE.md "Late changes after
46	   push") recurred three times during the 0.37.x dogfood — it's
47	   not a rare edge case, it's a workflow, and each manual run is
48	   a chance to miss a step or leave stale state. Once `--squash`
49	   exists, the "Late changes after push" section in CLAUDE.md
50	   retires; everything goes through the same two approval gates.
51	
52	   **Safety requirements** (implement as part of the squash work):
53	   - Needs a `--force-with-lease`-equivalent. Without it, squash
54	     is a silent history eraser if someone else pushed between
55	     our last fetch and our force-push.
56	   - The review gate should surface "will rewrite commit X → Y
57	     and force-push to remote" so the user's approval is about
58	     the rewrite, not just the diff.
59	   - Stage-prereq checks (separate todo entry above): squash needs
60	     to verify `@-` is actually the commit we're squashing, not
61	     something stale from a prior aborted run. Ties into the
62	     state-sanity preflight todo.
63	
64	   **Recommended ordering** (squash builds on earlier todos):
65	   1. State-sanity preflight on resume (earlier todo).
66	   2. Stage-prereq verification + honest completion (earlier todo).
67	   3. `--scope=app|claude|both` (simpler, independent).
68	   4. `--squash` (the big one; benefits from 1–3 being solid).
69	 - "Oh shit" revert — post-success undo via `.vc-x1-ops/`. Just an
70	   idea at this stage. Sketch: every repo-mutating command drops an
71	   anchor (timestamp, command+args, per-repo pre-op-id, per-repo
72	   pre-push remote ref snapshot) into a workspace-root `.vc-x1-ops/`
73	   directory. `vc-x1 undo` restores both repos to the anchored op
74	   via `jj op restore` and force-pushes remotes back to the anchored
75	   ref. Piggybacks on jj's native op-log retention (~30d default) for
76	   local restore; snapshots remote refs separately since jj's op log
77	   is local-only. Safety: TTL or generation cap on anchors, same
78	   `--force-with-lease`-equivalent as `--squash`, explicit confirm
79	   (destructive). Scope-aware (`vc-x1 undo --scope=app`). Generalizes
80	   beyond push — sync / finalize / init / fix-desc could all drop
81	   anchors. `.vc-x1-ops/` needs the same init + fixture auto-write +
82	   `.gitignore` discipline as `.vc-x1/`. Motivation: the common
83	   operator-error failure mode (wrong cwd, wrong scope, wrong
84	   thing) currently has no built-in recovery — just the error-prone
85	   manual `jj op restore` + `jj git push --allow-backwards` dance.
86	 - bm-track "silent when clean" refinement. Always probe on entry
87	   and exit, but only *print* when the state isn't fully tracked or
88	   the exit state differs from entry. **When we do print on exit,
89	   include the entry state in the output** so the transition is
90	   explicit rather than inferred — "entry was ok (not printed), exit
91	   failed" is inference; "entry=tracked, exit=NOT_TRACKED" in one
92	   printed line is evidence. Record entry state in a local variable
93	   at the enter call site and pass into the exit call. Output shape:
94	   `bm-track vc-x1 <cmd>: enter=<state> → exit=<state>` — one
95	   line only when there's something to say. Preserves detection
96	   value, removes the steady-state two-lines-per-command noise, and
97	   keeps provenance unambiguous. Deferred in 0.37.4 until
98	   signal-confidence is established through more varied dogfood —
99	   changing output behavior before then would muddy the "is the
100	   probe itself reliable?" question.
101	 - `vc-x1 push`: state-sanity preflight on resume. Before any stage runs,
102	   verify saved state matches reality: `state.app_chid` still exists
103	   (not abandoned/rewritten)?  `main` bookmark at `state.app_chid`'s
104	   commit? `state.claude_chid` consistent with `.claude` working copy?
105	   On mismatch, refuse with a loud "state is stale — run `vc-x1 push
106	   --restart`" error. Surfaced 2026-04-22: 0.37.1 push errored at
107	   finalize-claude; out-of-band recovery (manual finalize + force-push
108	   of a squash) moved the world forward; 0.37.2 push then resumed at
109	   the parked finalize-claude, no-op'd, and falsely declared "completed
110	   all stages" while working copies still held uncommitted changes.
111	 - `vc-x1 push`: stage-prereq verification + honest completion. Each
112	   stage declares what it expects (working-copy dirty for commit-app;
113	   bookmark at specific commit for bookmark-both; etc.); dispatcher
114	   checks before running. "Completed all stages" should only print
115	   when stages genuinely ran or were verified-already-done, not when
116	   they were skipped without verification. Same dogfood surfaced this.
117	 - CLAUDE.md "Manual finalize fallback": document that push's state
118	   file must be cleared (`vc-x1 push --restart` or `rm`) after any
119	   out-of-band recovery (manual `vc-x1 finalize`, `jj squash
120	   --ignore-immutable` + force-push, etc.) so a later `vc-x1 push`
121	   doesn't resume from a now-stale halt point.
122	 - `vc-x1 test-fixture` should refuse `--path` values that resolve inside
123	   the current workspace root (error or warn). Dogfood surfaced this:
124	   `--path ./tf-1` inside the repo let jj snapshot the fixture's bare-git
125	   remotes into the commit — a 56-file noise blob that got force-push'd
126	   off the remote later. Tool-level prevention, not `.gitignore`.
127	 - Tighten CLAUDE.md "Late changes after push" recipe: drop the
128	   `jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .` line when the squash target
129	   is `@-` (bookmark follows naturally). Keep it only for the cases
130	   where squash lands somewhere the bookmark isn't already.
131	 - Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection across every repo-modifying
132	   command. Diagnosed 2026-04-22 dogfood: jj's tracking state is
133	   **per-workspace** (local `.jj` store), not shared via git refs.
134	   Sync across machines transfers refs but not the tracking flag —
135	   so a fresh workspace fetched-into never auto-tracks. The failure
136	   surfaces only when `jj git push` is attempted, which is too late
137	   (push-app already succeeded in our case).
138	
139	   **Policy (decided):** error loudly, with the exact
140	   `jj bookmark track <b> --remote=<r> -R <repo>` remediation
141	   command. No self-heal — keeps the fix explicit and visible.
142	
143	   **Scope (every command that creates or mutates repo state):**
144	   - `vc-x1 init`: already correct (Step 10 calls `jj bookmark track`).
145	     Add the check as a post-condition sanity assertion anyway.
146	   - `vc-x1 clone`: does `git clone` then `jj git init --colocate`.
147	     Whether that combination auto-establishes tracking in jj's
148	     workspace store is unclear; probably needs an explicit
149	     `jj bookmark track` for each cloned bookmark after the init.
150	     Verify and fix.
151	   - `vc-x1 test-fixture`: Step 7's `jj git push --bookmark main`
152	     establishes tracking as a side effect of the first push. Works
153	     correctly — confirmed via `jj bookmark list --tracked` on a
154	     fresh fixture. No change needed; the post-condition sanity
155	     check would naturally cover it.
156	   - `vc-x1 sync` preflight: detect + error.
157	   - `vc-x1 push` preflight: detect + error (before any mutation).
158	   - `vc-x1 finalize`: detect + error before the squash, so a
159	     failed push doesn't leave a half-finalized state.
160	
161	   Shared helper: `common::verify_tracking(repo, bookmark, remote)
162	   -> Result<(), Err>` or similar. Probably use
163	   `jj bookmark list --tracked -T <template>` under the hood
164	   rather than parsing human-readable output.
165	 - Allow `vc-x1 push` to work on code or bot repo together or independantly and
166	   specifically we should be able to "squash" in th code repo just as we do in
167	   the bot repo.
168	 - Richer bookmark enumeration: per-bookmark remote presence + tracking status [52]

## Capture squash-mode + scope design for push (0.37.4)

After force-pushing 0.37.3 through the manual sequence (jj
squash --ignore-immutable → jj describe → jj git push on both
repos), the user asked: could `vc-x1 push` do this end-to-end
with the right flags? Answer — yes, and it's worth the effort.
This chore is doc-only: captures the design framing into
`notes/todo.md` so we don't lose it.

Two flag additions sketched:

1. `--scope=app|claude|both` (default `both`). Lets push run
   against one repo singly. Self-contained, independent of
   squash.
2. `--squash`. Squashes WC into `@-` via `--ignore-immutable`
   and force-pushes. Message stage pre-fills `$EDITOR` with
   the existing description. Motivation: the manual force-push
   sequence recurred three times during the 0.37.x dogfood
   (tf-1 removal, then 0.37.4-rolled-into-0.37.3 for the
   bm-track work, then would-have-been-again if we'd rolled
   THIS chore into 0.37.3). Not a rare edge case — a workflow.

Safety requirements for squash (captured in the todo entry):
`--force-with-lease`-equivalent, review gate surfaces the
rewrite (not just the diff), stage-prereq checks verify `@-`
is the commit we think it is.

Recommended ordering (earlier todos listed above this one):
state-sanity preflight → stage-prereq verification → `--scope`
(simpler, independent) → `--squash` (benefits from the first
three being solid).

- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — `0.37.3` → `0.37.4`.
- `notes/todo.md` — two new Todo entries under the push flag
  section: `--scope=app|claude|both` and `--squash`. The squash
  entry embeds the safety requirements + recommended ordering
  inline so future work has the framing without needing to
  re-derive it from chat transcripts.

Also captured in the same todo.md pass (follow-up conversation
after the initial framing):

- `--scope` entry grew a "warn on scope/WC mismatch" note —
  non-fatal warning when `--scope=app` leaves `.claude` pending
  changes on the table (or vice versa). Catches "I meant
  `--scope=both`" before commit.
- New Todo: "oh shit" revert via a `.vc-x1-ops/` anchor dir.
  Idea-stage only. Every repo-mutating command drops an anchor
  (command, per-repo pre-op-id, per-repo pre-push remote ref
  snapshot). `vc-x1 undo` restores via `jj op restore` +
  force-push to anchored remote ref. Piggybacks on jj's op-log
  retention for local, snapshots remotes separately. Generalizes
  beyond push (sync / finalize / init / fix-desc all eligible).
  Needs the same init + fixture + `.gitignore` discipline as
  `.vc-x1/`. Full framing lives in the todo entry.

## Capture `--message-file` design for push (0.37.5)

Surfaced during the 0.37.4 push itself — passing a long
multi-line body via `--title "$T" --body "$B"` shell variables
makes the actual `vc-x1 push …` invocation visually disappear
under the variable assignments when the whole thing is rendered
as one tool call. The immediate workaround (two-call pattern:
write title/body to temp files in one Bash call, then a short
`vc-x1 push … --title "$(cat t)" --body "$(cat b)"` in the
next) is zero-code-change and adopted starting this commit.

The tool-level fix sketched into `notes/todo.md`:

- **`vc-x1 push --message-file PATH`**. Git-style commit-message
  file (first line = title, blank, rest = body). Alternative
  to `--title` + `--body`. No short form — usage is infrequent
  enough that a long name reads cleanly.

Doc-only commit:

- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — `0.37.4` → `0.37.5`.
- `notes/todo.md` — new Todo entry for `--message-file`.
- `notes/chores-05.md` — this subsection.
```

1	# CLAUDE.md - Bot Instructions
2	
3	## Project Structure
4	
5	This project uses **two separate jj-git repos**:
6	
7	1. **App repo** (`/` — project root): Contains the application source code.
8	2. **Bot session repo** (`/.claude/`): Contains Claude Code session data.
9	
10	Both repos are managed with `jj` (Jujutsu), which coexists with git.
11	
12	## Repo Paths (relative from project root)
13	
14	- App repo: `.` (project root)
15	- Bot session repo: `.claude`
16	  (symlink from `~/.claude/projects/<path-to-project-root>/.claude`)
17	
18	## Working Directory
19	
20	Prefer staying in the project root. Use `-R` flags or absolute paths
21	to target other directories rather than `cd`. If `cd` seems necessary,
22	discuss with the user first — losing track of cwd causes subtle
23	command failures downstream.
24	
25	## Memory
26	
27	Do not use the bot's per-project memory directory
28	(`~/.claude/projects/<path>/memory/`). In a dual-repo setup with
29	CLAUDE.md it provides no capability CLAUDE.md doesn't already cover,
30	and it loses on discoverability:
31	
32	- **CLAUDE.md** — at the repo root, a well-known location for bot
33	  instructions, committed, reviewable, visible to every collaborator
34	  (human or bot).
35	- **Memory directory** — hidden under the user's home, tied to one
36	  machine, invisible to anyone but the bot, never diffed or reviewed.
37	
38	Easy for everyone to find beats convenient for the bot alone. Put
39	durable context in CLAUDE.md (or committed `notes/`) instead.
40	
41	## Speculation marker
42	
43	Durable text the bot writes — CLAUDE.md, `notes/`, commit bodies,
44	chores sections — should stick to observations and direct descriptions
45	of the code or data. If a mechanism, hypothesis, or causal claim
46	enters the text, prefix it with "The bot thinks ..." so a reader can
47	tell the measured from the inferred.
48	
49	**Why:** unmarked speculation reads like evidence, and a future reader
50	(or the bot on a later session) can pick it up as a known fact when
51	it's not. Measured / inferred is a distinction worth keeping visible
52	in the written record.
53	
54	**How to apply:** observations and factual descriptions need no
55	marker. Prefix with "The bot thinks ..." (or a close variant like
56	"The bot's guess is ...") when the claim is a mechanism ("X wins
57	because Y caches better"), a cause ("the drift was due to thermal
58	state"), a prediction ("this should scale linearly"), or any
59	reasoning not directly supported by the data on hand.
60	
61	## Committing
62	
63	Use `-R` (`--repository`) at the end to target the correct repo. Use
64	relative paths to reduce noise. Putting `-R` last keeps the verb/action
65	visible at the start of the command.
66	
67	### App repo
68	```
69	jj commit -m \
70	"title" \
71	-m "body
72	
73	ochid: /.claude/<changeID>" \
74	-R .
75	```
76	
77	### Bot session repo
78	```
79	jj commit -m \
80	"title" \
81	-m "body
82	
83	ochid: /<changeID>" \
84	-R .claude
85	```
86	
87	## jj Basics
88	
89	- `jj st -R .` / `jj st -R .claude` — show working copy status
90	- `jj log -R .` / `jj log -R .claude` — show commit log
91	- `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — finalize working copy into a commit
92	- `jj describe -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — set description without committing
93	- `jj git push --bookmark <name> -R <repo>` — push a bookmark (no
94	  `--allow-new` flag; jj pushes new bookmarks without special flags)
95	- In jj, the working copy (@) is always a mutable commit being edited.
96	  `jj commit` finalizes it and creates a new empty working copy on top.
97	- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active
98	  session because session data updates continuously.
99	
100	## Commit Message Style
101	
102	Use [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with
103	a version suffix:
104	
105	```
106	<type>: <short description> (<version>)
107	```
108	
109	- **Title**: target ~50 chars, short summary of *what* changed.
110	  Include the version. Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`,
111	  `test`, `docs`, `chore`.
112	- **App-repo body**: short intro paragraph (1–3 sentences), then a
113	  terse bullet list. Each bullet corresponds one-to-one with the
114	  edits structure already documented in `notes/chores-*.md` for
115	  this step — just the file and a one-line gist (e.g.
116	  `README.md: new Overview intro`). Do *not* restate the detail
117	  that lives in chores; the commit body is a scan-able index, not
118	  a duplicate. The chores section is the source of truth.
119	- **Session-repo body**: terse intro + a few session-activity
120	  bullets. Doesn't need to mirror chores since it describes
121	  in-session work, not code changes.
122	- Examples:
123	  - `feat: add fix-ochid subcommand (0.22.0)`
124	  - `fix: fix-ochid prefix bug (0.22.1)`
125	  - `refactor: deduplicate common CLI flags (0.21.1)`
126	
127	## Pre-commit Requirements
128	
129	### User approval
130	
131	Never execute commit, squash, push, or finalize commands without the
132	user's explicit approval. Present changes for review first; only run
133	them after the user confirms. This applies to late changes too —
134	pause for review before squashing into an existing commit.
135	
136	### Review before proposing the commit block
137	
138	After finishing a unit of work, **summarize what changed and stop
139	there**. Do not pre-emptively lay out the Checkpoint-1 commit
140	commands. Wait for the user to signal review is complete before
141	proposing the commit block. Changes during review are the norm,
142	not the exception; proposing commit text too early creates noise
143	and signals that I consider the work done when it usually isn't.
144	
145	This applies per-step in a multi-step flow too — each step gets a
146	review pause before its commit block appears.
147	
148	Signals that review is complete include explicit approval ("let's
149	commit", "looks good, commit it") **and any directive to start the
150	next step** ("do step 4", "next", "go N+1"). In that case the
151	previous step must be committed first — always commit the current
152	step before starting the next; don't ask.
153	
154	### Notes references
155	
156	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
157	See [Todo format](notes/README.md#todo-format) for details.
158	
159	### Versioning
160	
161	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based
162	on scope:
163	
164	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump
165	  directly to `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
166	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-0`,
167	  implement across multiple commits incrementing the numeric
168	  suffix. The final commit drops the suffix.
169	
170	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before
171	starting.
172	
173	For multi-step:
174	
175	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-0` with the plan and commit as a chore
176	   marker.
177	2. Implement in one or more `X.Y.Z-N` commits (increment N as
178	   needed).
179	3. Final commit bumps to `X.Y.Z` (no suffix), updates
180	   `notes/todo.md` and `notes/chores-*.md` — this is the "done"
181	   marker.
182	
183	**Why numeric suffixes (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than `-devN`:**
184	semver pre-release identifiers may consist of a single numeric
185	component, and they compare numerically per spec. So
186	`X.Y.Z-1 < X.Y.Z-2 < … < X.Y.Z` correctly orders the dev ladder
187	below the done marker. Cargo accepts this form. The `-dev` prefix
188	adds no information the git log doesn't already convey and
189	doubles typing per commit.
190	
191	The final release commit (no suffix) signals completion rather than
192	amending prior commits. This keeps history readable and makes it easy
193	to see which commits were exploratory vs final.
194	
195	### Chores section headers
196	
197	Chores section headers use trailing version format:
198	
199	```
200	## Description (X.Y.Z)
201	```
202	
203	Example: `` ## Add `fn claude-symlink` (0.27.0) ``
204	
205	### Pre-commit checklist
206	
207	Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
208	
209	1. `cargo fmt`
210	2. `cargo clippy`
211	3. `cargo test`
212	4. `cargo install --path .` (if applicable)
213	5. Retest after install
214	6. Update `notes/todo.md` — add to `## Done` if completing a task
215	7. Update `notes/chores-*.md` — add a subsection describing the change
216	8. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new flags,
217	   new subcommands, changed behavior)
218	
219	## Code Conventions
220	
221	### Doc comments on every file, function, and method
222	
223	Every `.rs` file must begin with a `//!` module docstring. Every
224	function and method must have a `///` doc comment. Keep them brief —
225	one sentence of purpose is often enough; the discipline is that the
226	comment exists, not that it be long.
227	
228	This is a deliberate override of the generic "write no comments"
229	default that applies to inline `//` comments. Doc comments on the
230	module / item surface are expected; inline explanatory comments
231	inside function bodies remain discouraged unless they capture a
232	non-obvious WHY.
233	
234	### `// OK: …` comments on `unwrap*` calls (Rust)
235	
236	Non-test code that calls `.unwrap()`, `.unwrap_or(…)`,
237	`.unwrap_or_default()`, or `.unwrap_or_else(…)` must have a trailing
238	`// OK: …` comment that justifies why the call is acceptable.
239	
240	- `// OK: <specific reason>` — document the real precondition,
241	  invariant, or domain reason. Preferred whenever the reason isn't
242	  self-evident.
243	- `// OK: obvious` — the default is self-evident from context (e.g.
244	  `desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("")` — empty desc → empty title).
245	
246	Bare `// OK` is not used (reads like a truncated comment).
247	Abbreviations (e.g. `SE`) are not used because they require a decoder
248	ring for readers seeing the code out of context.
249	
250	For provably-unreachable `.unwrap()` calls, also prefix with
251	`#[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]` so the site stays silent if we enable
252	the project-wide `clippy::unwrap_used` lint later.
253	
254	```rust
255	let max = stderr_level.unwrap_or(LevelFilter::Info); // OK: default verbosity when -v/-vv absent
256	let first_line = desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("");  // OK: obvious
257	
258	match matches.len() {
259	    1 => {
260	        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
261	        // OK: `1 =>` arm guarantees matches.len() == 1
262	        Ok(TitleMatch::One(matches.into_iter().next().unwrap()))
263	    }
264	    // ...
265	}
266	```
267	
268	Tests (`#[cfg(test)]`) are exempt — panicking on setup failure is the
269	correct test behavior.
270	
271	### Ask for clarification on ambiguous input
272	
273	When user input is ambiguous or missing necessary detail, stop and
274	ask a specific question. Do not proceed on a guess and hope the
275	result lands right — a clarifying question costs a few seconds;
276	redoing misaligned work costs much more.
277	
278	### Recognize when stuck
279	
280	If a simple task has eaten 5+ minutes of thinking or back-and-forth
281	without progress, stop. Summarize what's blocking — unclear
282	requirements, unfamiliar API surface, conflicting signals — and ask.
283	Continued flailing produces worse outcomes than a direct "I'm stuck
284	on X."
285	
286	## ochid Trailers
287	
288	Every commit body must include an `ochid:` trailer pointing to the
289	counterpart commit in the other repo. The value is a workspace-root-relative
290	path followed by the changeID:
291	
292	- App repo commits point to `.claude`: `ochid: /.claude/<changeID>`
293	- Bot session commits point to app repo: `ochid: /<changeID>`
294	
295	Use `vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L` to get both changeIDs (first line
296	is app repo, second is `.claude`).
297	
298	## Commit-Push-Finalize Flow
299	
300	**Use `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` — it wraps the full flow in one
301	command with two interactive approval gates.** Push runs
302	preflight (sync + fmt + clippy + test), prompts for review,
303	composes the commit message (via `--title`/`--body` or `$EDITOR`),
304	prompts for message approval, then commits both repos → advances
305	bookmarks → pushes app → finalizes `.claude`. Failures inside the
306	local mutation window roll both repos back via `jj op restore`;
307	after `push-app` succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and
308	recovery is forward-only.
309	
310	**Run this flow after every step** — not only at session end.
311	Single-step and multi-step changes are of equal importance: a
312	single-step change is one `push` invocation; a multi-step change
313	is one `push` per `X.Y.Z-N` commit plus one for the final release
314	commit. Each step gets its own commit, its own push, and its own
315	finalize — so dev markers land on the remote and in `.claude` as
316	they happen rather than being batched until the end.
317	
318	### Run `vc-x1 push`
319	
320	```
321	vc-x1 push main                                      # interactive (review + $EDITOR)
322	vc-x1 push main --title "..." --body "..."           # flags skip $EDITOR
323	vc-x1 push main --yes --title "..." --body "..."     # full non-interactive
324	vc-x1 push main --dry-run                            # preview (no side effects)
325	vc-x1 push main --from commit-app                    # resume from specific stage
326	vc-x1 push --status                                  # show saved state
327	vc-x1 push main --restart                            # clear saved state; start fresh
328	```
329	
330	The two approval gates are surfaced by push itself:
331	
332	1. **Review** — push prints `jj diff --stat` for both repos and
333	   prompts `[y/N]`. Approve = "the work is done right".
334	2. **Message** — push either uses `--title`/`--body` (non-editor
335	   path) or opens `$EDITOR` on a template. Approve = "the message
336	   reads right".
337	
338	Both titles and bodies are the **same** across the two commits;
339	only the `ochid:` trailer differs per repo. Push collects the
340	pre-commit chids internally so you don't hand-manage them.
341	
342	For the full flag list and stage machine, see `vc-x1 push --help`
343	and `notes/chores-05.md > Add push subcommand (0.37.0)`.
344	
345	### Bot communication during the flow
346	
347	When applying the flow on the user's behalf, use plain prose at
348	each gate — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal", "Checkpoint N",
349	etc.):
350	
351	1. **After completing the work** — summarize what changed
352	   (file-by-file or feature-by-feature, terse) and end with
353	   something like:
354	
355	   > Work complete. Please review. On approval I'll prep the
356	   > commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.
357	
358	2. **After review approval** — present the proposed `$TITLE` and
359	   `$BODY` explicitly, then ask permission to run `vc-x1 push`.
360	   Do **not** spell out the full
361	   `vc-x1 push <bookmark> --yes --title "$TITLE" --body "$BODY"`
362	   invocation by default — it's mechanical and obvious from the
363	   title/body. Show it only on explicit request (verbose mode /
364	   debugging).
365	
366	3. **After execution approval** — run the push command. `push`
367	   handles commit + bookmark + push + finalize internally;
368	   nothing should be output after the push command (finalize is
369	   detached and absolute-last; see "After finalize: stop and
370	   wait" below).
371	
372	### Pre-step: `vc-x1 sync` (still useful)
373	
374	Push's preflight runs `vc-x1 sync --check` as its first step. Check
375	mode verifies divergence without applying — if anything is `behind`
376	or `diverged`, preflight errors and you resolve explicitly with
377	`vc-x1 sync --no-check` before re-running push. This keeps the
378	two approval gates (review, message) authoritative; push never
379	performs an unsupervised rebase.
380	
381	Running sync manually before you *start* editing is still cheap
382	(one line when clean) and surfaces remote changes earlier:
383	
384	```
385	vc-x1 sync --check                    # dual-repo workspace, verify
386	vc-x1 sync --no-check                 # apply (rebase / fast-forward)
387	vc-x1 sync --check -R .               # single-repo project
388	vc-x1 sync --check --quiet            # silent; exit code signals result
389	```
390	
391	Bots and scripts must pass `--check` or `--no-check` explicitly —
392	defaults can shift, explicit flags lock in the contract. Interactive
393	use can take the default (which is `--check`).
394	
395	Output shape:
396	
397	- **Clean**: one line — `sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`.
398	  Note: scope is bookmark-vs-remote tracking, not working-copy
399	  cleanliness — `@` may have uncommitted changes; sync doesn't
400	  speak to that. Proceed.
401	- **Action needed** (`behind` / `diverged`) under `--check`:
402	  per-repo fetch + state lines, then a fatal
403	  `sync: N repos need action — resolve with vc-x1 sync --no-check
404	  and re-run`. Inspect, run `vc-x1 sync --no-check`, then proceed.
405	- **`--quiet`**: no output; exit code is the only signal.
406	
407	### After finalize: stop and wait
408	
409	After `vc-x1 finalize` is launched — **whether mid-session per-step
410	or at session end** — you **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step, edit
411	files, run tools, or emit any text (prose, recaps, acknowledgements),
412	until the user explicitly directs you to continue. Treat finalize as
413	a hard stop for the whole turn. Any final words (e.g. "next is ...")
414	must be said in the approval prompt *before* executing finalize; the
415	finalize `Bash` call is the last thing in the turn and nothing
416	follows it.
417	
418	This holds even when the next step seems obvious (e.g. "next is
419	N+1" or "now I should bump the version and commit the release").
420	Wait. The user controls cadence — every push+finalize is a checkpoint
421	they may want to inspect, think about, hand off, or take a break at.
422	Auto-proceeding bypasses that checkpoint and produces unwanted writes
423	between finalize and the next explicit instruction.
424	
425	Exceptions to this rule may emerge later but are not authorized at
426	this stage. Until told otherwise, treat as absolute.
427	
428	### Late changes after push
429	
430	If the app repo needs a tweak after `push-app` succeeded (e.g.
431	updating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is immutable. Use
432	`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes in, then re-push:
433	
434	```
435	jj squash --ignore-immutable -R .
436	jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
437	jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
438	```
439	
440	(`.claude` is also mutable via this pattern when needed, though
441	push's `finalize-claude` stage normally handles trailing session
442	writes so you rarely hit this case there.)
443	
444	### Manual finalize fallback
445	
446	If push exited before `finalize-claude` (e.g. `--no-finalize`
447	was set, or a failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`),
448	run finalize by hand:
449	
450	```
451	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
452	```
453	
454	**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no
455	tool calls, no additional output. If any work is done after
456	finalize, run finalize again so the trailing writes are captured.
457	Do **not** echo or restate the finalize output — the Bash tool
458	already displays it.
459	
```

  "new_string": "### Notes references\n\nMultiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.\nSee [Todo format](notes/README.md#todo-format) for details.\n\n### Markdown anchor links\n\nGitHub-style anchors (used when linking from `notes/todo.md` into\n`notes/chores-*.md` headers, and similar cross-file refs) are\ngenerated by this algorithm:\n\n1. Lowercase the header text.\n2. Drop punctuation **in place** — do not collapse adjacent\n   whitespace. So `:`, `.`, `(`, `)`, `+`, etc. are deleted but\n   the spaces around them stay put.\n3. Map each remaining space to one hyphen, 1-for-1.\n\nExamples:\n\n- `Add push subcommand (0.37.0)` →\n  `add-push-subcommand-0370`\n- `CLAUDE.md refresh + memory migration (0.36.1)` →\n  `claudemd-refresh--memory-migration-0361` — `+` dropped, its\n  *two* surrounding spaces remain → `--`.\n- `Open: sync up-to-date should mention working-copy state` →\n  `open-sync-up-to-date-should-mention-working-copy-state` — `:`\n  had only a trailing space, so the dropped `:` plus the one\n  surviving space maps to a single `-`, not `--`.\n\n**Why:** the single-vs-double-hyphen distinction is easy to get\nwrong on punctuation that has whitespace on only one side (e.g.\n`:` flush against the preceding word). A bad anchor is dead\nweight — the link doesn't resolve — and the breakage is silent\nin the rendered markdown.\n\n### Versioning"
}

Based on the search results, here's what I found about GitHub's markdown heading anchor algorithm and slug generation rules:

## Key Findings

GitHub generates slugs for markdown headings and ensures they are unique in a specific way. While the search results don't provide an official GitHub specification document, they do reveal some of the rules:

**Basic Slug Generation Rules:**
- Spaces are converted to hyphens
- Characters like á,é,í,ó,ú,Á,É,Í,Ó,Ú,Ñ,ñ are omitted in anchors

**Text Transformation:**
- Headings like "## Foo" become `<h2 id="foo">Foo</h2>` (converted to lowercase)

**Duplicate Handling:**
- GitHub ensures slugs are unique in the same way, though the exact mechanism isn't detailed in these results

## Notable Resources

The search results point to several implementations that attempt to replicate GitHub's behavior:
- The github-slugger library generates slugs just like GitHub does for markdown headings
- ToC links are compatible with anchors generated by GitHub's Markdown renderer, and mdformat-toc defaults to using GitHub's slug function

However, GitHub doesn't appear to publish an official detailed specification document. The rules are primarily inferred from observing GitHub's behavior and through reverse-engineering efforts by the community.

987	## Capture `--message-file` design for push (0.37.5)
988	
989	Surfaced during the 0.37.4 push itself — passing a long
990	multi-line body via `--title "$T" --body "$B"` shell variables
991	makes the actual `vc-x1 push …` invocation visually disappear
992	under the variable assignments when the whole thing is rendered
993	as one tool call. The immediate workaround (two-call pattern:
994	write title/body to temp files in one Bash call, then a short
995	`vc-x1 push … --title "$(cat t)" --body "$(cat b)"` in the
996	next) is zero-code-change and adopted starting this commit.
997	
998	The tool-level fix sketched into `notes/todo.md`:
999	
1000	- **`vc-x1 push --message-file PATH`**. Git-style commit-message
1001	  file (first line = title, blank, rest = body). Alternative
1002	  to `--title` + `--body`. No short form — usage is infrequent
1003	  enough that a long name reads cleanly.
1004

This looks like an external auth problem (SSH agent, credential, VPN, server hiccup) rather than anything in our 0.37.6 changes. A few diagnostic options if you want me to dig:

- `git -C .claude remote -v` and `git -C . remote -v` — confirm remotes look right
- `jj git fetch -R .claude` directly — see if the error reproduces outside push
- `ssh -T <host>` for the relevant remote — check auth standalone

```
 - Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection across every repo-modifying
   command. Diagnosed 2026-04-22 dogfood: jj's tracking state is
   **per-workspace** (local `.jj` store), not shared via git refs.
   Sync across machines transfers refs but not the tracking flag —
   so a fresh workspace fetched-into never auto-tracks. The failure
   surfaces only when `jj git push` is attempted, which is too late
   (push-app already succeeded in our case).

   **Policy (decided):** error loudly, with the exact
   `jj bookmark track <b> --remote=<r> -R <repo>` remediation
   command. No self-heal — keeps the fix explicit and visible.

   **Scope (every command that creates or mutates repo state):**
   - `vc-x1 init`: already correct (Step 10 calls `jj bookmark track`).
     Add the check as a post-condition sanity assertion anyway.
   - `vc-x1 clone`: does `git clone` then `jj git init --colocate`.
     Whether that combination auto-establishes tracking in jj's
     workspace store is unclear; probably needs an explicit
     `jj bookmark track` for each cloned bookmark after the init.
     Verify and fix.
   - `vc-x1 test-fixture`: Step 7's `jj git push --bookmark main`
     establishes tracking as a side effect of the first push. Works
     correctly — confirmed via `jj bookmark list --tracked` on a
     fresh fixture. No change needed; the post-condition sanity
     check would naturally cover it.
   - `vc-x1 sync` preflight: detect + error.
   - `vc-x1 push` preflight: detect + error (before any mutation).
   - `vc-x1 finalize`: detect + error before the squash, so a
     failed push doesn't leave a half-finalized state.

   Shared helper: `common::verify_tracking(repo, bookmark, remote)
   -> Result<(), Err>` or similar. Probably use
   `jj bookmark list --tracked -T <template>` under the hood
   rather than parsing human-readable output.
 - Allow `vc-x1 push` to work on code or bot repo together or independantly and
   specifically we should be able to "squash" in th code repo just as we do in
   the bot repo.
 - Richer bookmark enumeration: per-bookmark remote presence + tracking status [52]
 - Per-line/per-thread runtime log points (future, maybe) [36]
 - Add Windows symlink support via `std::os::windows::fs::symlink_dir` [37]
 - Add "::" revision syntax for jj compatibility
 - Add -p, --parents, -c, --children so parent and child counts can be asymmetric
 - Add integration tests in tests/ for subcommands using temp jj repos (tempfile crate)
 - Fix .claude repo history: dev0 through dev2 sessions squashed into wrong commit [4],[5]
 - Add `vc-x1 setup` subcommand: completions install, .claude repo init, symlink setup [27]
 - Add dynamic revision completion via `ArgValueCompleter` (jj doesn't complete revsets either) [28],[29]

Probably: alias for one minor cycle,\ndeprecate, remove. Decide before implementation.\n\n## Push hardening: state + stage sanity (design)\n\nTwo related correctness fixes for `vc-x1 push` resume behavior.\nBoth surfaced 2026-04-22 from a 0.37.1 dogfood incident and are\ndescribed together because they tackle the same failure mode\n(silent false success after stale state).\n\n**The incident:** 0.37.1 push errored at finalize-claude;\nout-of-band recovery (manual finalize + force-push of a squash)\nmoved the world forward; 0.37.2 push then resumed at the parked\nfinalize-claude, no-op'd, and falsely declared \"completed all\nstages\" while working copies still held uncommitted changes.\n\n**State-sanity preflight on resume.** Before any stage runs,\nverify saved state matches reality:\n\n- `state.app_chid` still exists (not abandoned/rewritten).\n- `main` bookmark at `state.app_chid`'s commit.\n- `state.claude_chid` consistent with `.claude` working copy.\n\nOn mismatch, refuse with a loud \"state is stale — run\n`vc-x1 push --restart`\" error.\n\n**Stage-prereq verification + honest completion.** Each stage\ndeclares what it expects (working-copy dirty for commit-app;\nbookmark at specific commit for bookmark-both; etc.); the\ndispatcher checks before running. \"Completed all stages\" should\nonly print when stages genuinely ran or were\nverified-already-done, not when they were skipped without\nverification.\n\n**Implementation order:** state-sanity first (broader guard),\nthen per-stage prereqs (more invasive). Both should land before\nthe `--squash` work to avoid building on a known-fragile resume\npath.\n\n## bm-track silent-when-clean (design)\n\nToday bm-track prints two lines per command (entry + exit)\nunconditionally. In steady state both lines say\n`tracked,tracked` — pure noise. Defer-flagged in 0.37.4 until\nsignal-confidence was established through more dogfood; several\n0.37.x cycles have now used the probe with no false positives\nor missed regressions.\n\n**Proposed behavior:**\n\n- Always probe on entry and exit (no change).\n- Print on entry only when state isn't fully tracked.\n- Print on exit when state isn't fully tracked OR when the exit\n  state differs from entry.\n- When printing on exit, include the entry state in the output\n  so the transition is explicit:\n  `bm-track vc-x1 <cmd>: enter=<state> → exit=<state>`.\n\nThe entry-state-in-exit-output requirement is important —\n\"entry was ok (not printed), exit failed\" is inference;\n\"entry=tracked, exit=NOT_TRACKED\" in one line is evidence.\n\n**Implementation note:** record entry state in a local variable\nat the enter call site and pass into the exit call.\n\n## Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection (design)\n\nDiagnosed 2026-04-22 dogfood: jj's tracking state is\n**per-workspace** (local `.jj` store), not shared via git refs.\nSync across machines transfers refs but not the tracking flag —\nso a fresh workspace fetched-into never auto-tracks. The failure\nsurfaces only when `jj git push` is attempted, which is too late\n(push-app already succeeded in our case).\n\n**Policy (decided):** error loudly, with the exact\n`jj bookmark track <b> --remote=<r> -R <repo>` remediation\ncommand. No self-heal — keeps the fix explicit and visible.\n\n**Scope (every command that creates or mutates repo state):**\n\n- `vc-x1 init`: already correct (Step 10 calls `jj bookmark\n  track`). Add the check as a post-condition sanity assertion\n  anyway.\n- `vc-x1 clone`: does `git clone` then `jj git init --colocate`.\n  Whether that combination auto-establishes tracking in jj's\n  workspace store is unclear; probably needs an explicit\n  `jj bookmark track` for each cloned bookmark after the init.\n  Verify and fix.\n- `vc-x1 test-fixture`: Step 7's `jj git push --bookmark main`\n  establishes tracking as a side effect of the first push.\n  Works correctly — confirmed via `jj bookmark list --tracked`\n  on a fresh fixture. No change needed; the post-condition\n  sanity check would naturally cover it.\n- `vc-x1 sync` preflight: detect + error.\n- `vc-x1 push` preflight: detect + error (before any mutation).\n- `vc-x1 finalize`: detect + error before the squash, so a\n  failed push doesn't leave a half-finalized state.\n\n**Shared helper:** `common::verify_tracking(repo, bookmark,\nremote) -> Result<(), Err>` or similar. Probably use\n`jj bookmark list --tracked -T <template>` under the hood\nrather than parsing human-readable output.\n"
}

11	A markdown list of task to do in the near feature
12	
13	 - I'd like vc-x1 to work with single and well as dual repo for all commands
14	   One technique we've talked about below for push is to add a "scope" option
15	   like:
16	     - --scope=app
17	     - --scope=other
18	     - --scope=both
19	 - sync: surface working-copy state in the up-to-date summary (per-repo
20	   pending-files count or compact stat). Wording-only fix shipped in
21	   0.37.1; this is the design+impl of the actual signal. [54]
22	 - Add `status` (alias `st`) subcommand: `jj st` across both repos in
23	   one shot. Natural home for the working-copy signal called out in [54].
24	 - Mirror `--check` / `--no-check` onto `vc-x1 push` (forwards through to
25	   the preflight `vc-x1 sync` invocation). 0.37.1 hard-codes `--check`;
26	   `--no-check` would be the user-opt-in to "auto-rebase under the gates,
27	   I trust the sync state". Default stays `--check`.
28	 - `vc-x1 push`: `--message-file PATH` flag. Reads a git-style commit
29	   message file — first line is title, blank line, rest is body.
30	   Equivalent to today's `--title "$(cat t)" --body "$(cat b)"` but
31	   one flag, one file, no shell escaping gymnastics. Motivation
32	   (2026-04-22): when scripting a push with a long multi-line body,
33	   the `--title "$T" --body "$B"` pattern makes the actual `vc-x1
34	   push …` invocation visually disappear under the variable
35	   assignments in terminal output — hard to scan. A file-based
36	   message keeps the invocation short. No short form (`-m`, `-F`,
37	   etc.) — infrequent enough that the long name is fine. Default `both` matches
38	   current behavior. `--scope=app` or `--scope=claude` runs push against
39	   one repo only — for docs-only fixes on the app side, or a `.claude`
40	   finalize that doesn't need to bundle app changes. Independent of
41	   the squash work below; simpler and self-contained. **Warn on
42	   scope/WC mismatch**: if `--scope=app` is chosen and `.claude` has
43	   pending changes (or vice versa), emit a non-fatal warning
44	   naming the pending files — catches the "I meant --scope=both"
45	   case before the user commits.
46	 - `vc-x1 push`: `--squash` flag. Instead of creating a new commit,
47	   squashes WC into the current repo's `@-` via `--ignore-immutable`
48	   and force-pushes. Message stage pre-fills `$EDITOR` with the
49	   existing description so typical use is edit-in-place; `--title`
50	   / `--body` still override. Motivation: the manual squash +
51	   describe + force-push sequence (CLAUDE.md "Late changes after
52	   push") recurred three times during the 0.37.x dogfood — it's
53	   not a rare edge case, it's a workflow, and each manual run is
54	   a chance to miss a step or leave stale state. Once `--squash`
55	   exists, the "Late changes after push" section in CLAUDE.md
56	   retires; everything goes through the same two approval gates.
57	
58	   **Safety requirements** (implement as part of the squash work):
59	   - Needs a `--force-with-lease`-equivalent. Without it, squash
60	     is a silent history eraser if someone else pushed between
61	     our last fetch and our force-push.
62	   - The review gate should surface "will rewrite commit X → Y
63	     and force-push to remote" so the user's approval is about
64	     the rewrite, not just the diff.
65	   - Stage-prereq checks (separate todo entry above): squash needs
66	     to verify `@-` is actually the commit we're squashing, not
67	     something stale from a prior aborted run. Ties into the
68	     state-sanity preflight todo.
69	
70	   **Recommended ordering** (squash builds on earlier todos):
71	   1. State-sanity preflight on resume (earlier todo).
72	   2. Stage-prereq verification + honest completion (earlier todo).
73	   3. `--scope=app|claude|both` (simpler, independent).
74	   4. `--squash` (the big one; benefits from 1–3 being solid).
75	 - "Oh shit" revert — post-success undo via `.vc-x1-ops/`. Just an
76	   idea at this stage. Sketch: every repo-mutating command drops an
77	   anchor (timestamp, command+args, per-repo pre-op-id, per-repo
78	   pre-push remote ref snapshot) into a workspace-root `.vc-x1-ops/`
79	   directory. `vc-x1 undo` restores both repos to the anchored op
80	   via `jj op restore` and force-pushes remotes back to the anchored
81	   ref. Piggybacks on jj's native op-log retention (~30d default) for
82	   local restore; snapshots remote refs separately since jj's op log
83	   is local-only. Safety: TTL or generation cap on anchors, same
84	   `--force-with-lease`-equivalent as `--squash`, explicit confirm
85	   (destructive). Scope-aware (`vc-x1 undo --scope=app`). Generalizes
86	   beyond push — sync / finalize / init / fix-desc could all drop
87	   anchors. `.vc-x1-ops/` needs the same init + fixture auto-write +
88	   `.gitignore` discipline as `.vc-x1/`. Motivation: the common
89	   operator-error failure mode (wrong cwd, wrong scope, wrong
90	   thing) currently has no built-in recovery — just the error-prone
91	   manual `jj op restore` + `jj git push --allow-backwards` dance.
92	 - bm-track "silent when clean" refinement. Always probe on entry
93	   and exit, but only *print* when the state isn't fully tracked or
94	   the exit state differs from entry. **When we do print on exit,
95	   include the entry state in the output** so the transition is
96	   explicit rather than inferred — "entry was ok (not printed), exit
97	   failed" is inference; "entry=tracked, exit=NOT_TRACKED" in one
98	   printed line is evidence. Record entry state in a local variable
99	   at the enter call site and pass into the exit call. Output shape:
100	   `bm-track vc-x1 <cmd>: enter=<state> → exit=<state>` — one
101	   line only when there's something to say. Preserves detection
102	   value, removes the steady-state two-lines-per-command noise, and
103	   keeps provenance unambiguous. Deferred in 0.37.4 until
104	   signal-confidence is established through more varied dogfood —
105	   changing output behavior before then would muddy the "is the
106	   probe itself reliable?" question.
107	 - `vc-x1 push`: state-sanity preflight on resume. Before any stage runs,
108	   verify saved state matches reality: `state.app_chid` still exists
109	   (not abandoned/rewritten)?  `main` bookmark at `state.app_chid`'s
110	   commit? `state.claude_chid` consistent with `.claude` working copy?
111	   On mismatch, refuse with a loud "state is stale — run `vc-x1 push
112	   --restart`" error. Surfaced 2026-04-22: 0.37.1 push errored at
113	   finalize-claude; out-of-band recovery (manual finalize + force-push
114	   of a squash) moved the world forward; 0.37.2 push then resumed at
115	   the parked finalize-claude, no-op'd, and falsely declared "completed
116	   all stages" while working copies still held uncommitted changes.
117	 - `vc-x1 push`: stage-prereq verification + honest completion. Each
118	   stage declares what it expects (working-copy dirty for commit-app;
119	   bookmark at specific commit for bookmark-both; etc.); dispatcher

126	   remotes into the commit — a 56-file noise blob that got force-push'd

131	   Sync across machines transfers refs but not the tracking flag —
132	   so a fresh workspace fetched-into never auto-tracks. The failure
133	   surfaces only when `jj git push` is attempted, which is too late

135	
136	   **Policy (decided):** error loudly, with the exact
137	   `jj bookmark track <b> --remote=<r> -R <repo>` remediation

140	   **Scope (every command that creates or mutates repo state):**
141	   - `vc-x1 init`: already correct (Step 10 calls `jj bookmark track`).

144	     Whether that combination auto-establishes tracking in jj's

152	     check would naturally cover it.
153	   - `vc-x1 sync` preflight: detect + error.
154	   - `vc-x1 push` preflight: detect + error (before any mutation).
155	   - `vc-x1 finalize`: detect + error before the squash, so a
156	     failed push doesn't leave a half-finalized state.

> DEVELOPER

Just came up with a trivial high priority change, the todo list should be numbered so I can say "Let's work on #1" I added two 1. for the first two is there a better way, I don't want to have to renumber.

> AGENT

Yes — "lazy numbering". Use `1.` for *every* item; CommonMark/GFM renderers auto-number regardless of the literal. Reorder or insert without re-numbering:

```
1. Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection ... [63]
1. vc-x1 push: state-sanity preflight ... [61]
1. vc-x1 push: stage-prereq verification ... [61]
1. vc-x1 commands: --scope generalization [60]
...
```

GitHub renders that as "1. 2. 3. 4. ...". Caveat: every item has to be `1.` — if you mix `-` bullets in, the numbered list breaks where the `-` starts.

Quick scope question before I apply:

- **Minimum change:** convert all `-` → `1.` in todo.md's Todo section (leave Done alone — no "#5 done" references).
- **Plus convention note:** add one line to `notes/README.md` under the `## Todo format` section documenting the lazy numbering.

Commit path:
- **Squash into 0.37.7** (via the "Late changes after push" recipe) — cohesive with the restructure theme, single force-push.
- **New 0.37.8** — clean separation.

My lean: minimum-change + README note, squashed into 0.37.7. OK to proceed, or pick a different combo?

> DEVELOPER

squash into 0.37.7, more dog fooding :)

> DEVELOPER

So two near term goals, ideally we should have been able to use vc-x1 to do the squash on both with one command `vc-x1 push --squash`, but secondarily if for some reason we didn't we should be able to do something like `vc-x1 push --squash --scope=other` or maybe `vc-x1 push --squash --other=.claude` i.e. one at a time, make sense?

> AGENT

Makes sense, and it falls out cleanly from the existing designs once you compose them:

**Primary** (`vc-x1 push --squash` with no scope): default scope is `both`, so this naturally squashes both repos and force-pushes both. End-to-end version of the manual two-step we just did. This is what the chores-05 #57 squash design implies once the chores-06 #60 scope generalization is in place.

**Secondary** (per-repo): `vc-x1 push --squash --scope=app` and `vc-x1 push --squash --scope=other` fall out from the same composition. Both shapes exist for free once the scope vocabulary is the project-wide convention. This is exactly the win [60] argues for — new flags get scope coverage automatically.

On `--other=.claude` vs `--scope=other`: I'd argue these solve different problems. `--scope=other` selects *which scope this command runs against* (per-command). `--other=PATH` would be saying *what "other" actually points to* (per-project). The latter is project config — should live in `.vc-config.toml` or similar — so the per-command flag stays small and consistent. If a project's "other" isn't `.claude`, the config sets it once and `--scope=other` works the same everywhere.

Two questions:

1. Proceed with the manual `.claude` squash now (close out the current dogfood), then in a follow-up I'll fold this composition note + `--other` config-vs-flag distinction into chores-05 #57 and chores-06 #60? Or hold the .claude side until after we capture the design?
2. Agree on `--other` belonging in config, not as a per-command flag? (If you'd rather have it as a flag too, say so before I write it up.)

> DEVELOPER

1. yes proceed
2. .vc-config.toml already has `other-repo` so that's covered but when we say `--scope=xxx` my interpretation is we're saying do the operation on the "specified" (direct/indirect) single repo.

> DEVELOPER

Do we agree that when we implement --scope and it's parameter is something other than "both" the operation is single repo?

> AGENT

Yes, agreed. With the current `--scope=app|other|both` vocabulary, the invariant is:

- `--scope=both` (or omitted, since `both` is the default) → dual-repo operation.
- `--scope=app` → single-repo operation on app.
- `--scope=other` → single-repo operation on other (resolved via `.vc-config.toml`'s `other-repo`).

Anything that isn't `both` is single-repo. Clean, and easy to encode as the dispatcher's branch point.

Want me to capture this confirmed semantic into chores-06 #60 next time we touch it (e.g. as part of a 0.37.8 design refinement commit)?

**Summary of 0.37.8:**

- `notes/chores-06.md` — new subsection `## Confirm scope semantic + squash composition (0.37.8)`. Captures three things from this turn's discussion:
  - **Scope semantic invariant.** `--scope=both` (default) → dual; anything else → single-repo. Direct (`app`) vs indirect (`other`, resolves via `[workspace].other-repo` in `.vc-config.toml`).
  - **`--other` is project config, not a per-command flag.** Already in `.vc-config.toml` as `[workspace].other-repo`. Per-command surface stays uniform.
  - **`vc-x1 push --squash` composition.** With scope in place, `--squash` (no scope) does both repos; `--scope=app` or `--scope=other` does one. Plus dogfood validation: ochid integrity preserved through `jj squash --ignore-immutable`, two-line recipe worked identically on both sides, push state was clean.
- `notes/todo.md` — Done entry + new `[65]` reference.

Please review. On approval I'll prep the commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.

> DEVELOPER

If we `cd .claude` then run `vc-x1 xxx --scope=app` what does that mean, I think it's `--scope=..` but it so happens that `.vc-config.toml::other-repo` so what does `--scope=other` mean while cwd is .claude?

> AGENT

Workspace-anchored, not cwd-relative. The semantic I'd argue for:

**vc-x1 finds the workspace by walking up from cwd to the directory containing `.vc-config.toml`** (the app repo root, which has `[workspace] path = "/" other-repo = ".claude"`). All scope names resolve against THAT workspace, regardless of where you cd to inside it.

So from `cd .claude`:
- `--scope=app` → app repo (workspace root). Happens to equal `..` from your cwd, but conceptually "the app repo, wherever it is".
- `--scope=other` → other repo (`[workspace].other-repo` = `.claude`). Happens to equal `.` from your cwd, but conceptually "the other repo, wherever it is".

So scope names are stable repo identities, not directional/relative. From any cwd in the workspace, `--scope=app` always means the same repo, and so does `--scope=other`. Same as how `git status` from any subdir reports the same repo.

Implication: there's only ONE `.vc-config.toml` per workspace (at the workspace root, currently the app repo). `.claude` doesn't have its own — it's part of the workspace described by the parent's config. The mental model is "workspace contains repos", not "each repo defines its own view".

This semantic is important enough to add to the chores-06 #65 design capture before we push 0.37.8 — it answers a "what happens when..." question that would otherwise come up during implementation. Want me to fold it in (single paragraph under the **Scope semantic invariant** section)?

```
# vc-config: Vibe Coding workspace configuration
#
# workspace-path is this repo's path relative to the workspace root.
# Used to resolve changeID paths in git trailers (e.g. ochid: /.claude/changeID).
# other-repo is the relative path to the counterpart repo.

[workspace]
path = "/.claude"
other-repo = ".."
```

> AGENT

Confirmed. The configs are symmetric self-descriptions:

- App: `path = "/"`, `other-repo = ".claude"`
- `.claude`: `path = "/.claude"`, `other-repo = ".."`

`path = "/"` is the marker for the app role — only the app has it. So from any cwd the dispatcher reads the local config, determines "am I app or other?" via `path`, then resolves the requested scope to the right physical path:

From `cd .claude`:
- `--scope=app` → `..` (resolved via .claude's `other-repo`, since I'm not app, the counterpart IS the app)
- `--scope=other` → `.` (this repo itself, since `path != "/"` → I AM the workspace's "other")

Scope semantics stay workspace-anchored — `--scope=app` always means the app repo, `--scope=other` always means the `.claude` repo, regardless of cwd. There's a naming gotcha worth flagging though:

- **Config field `other-repo`** = "this repo's counterpart" (cwd-flavored direction; from `.claude` it's `..` = the app)
- **CLI value `--scope=other`** = "the workspace's 'other' role" (workspace-anchored; always = `.claude`)

They coincide from app's cwd (both physically = `.claude`) but diverge from `.claude`'s cwd (config `other-repo` = `..`, CLI `--scope=other` = `.`). The two namespaces don't conflict because the dispatcher knows which it's resolving — but the shared word `other` will be a confusion magnet. Worth considering renaming the config field to `counterpart` or `peer` to disambiguate.

Want me to fold this clarification into chores-06 #65 (cwd-independence + naming gotcha + counterpart-rename suggestion) before pushing 0.37.8?

> DEVELOPER

Also, what should vc-x1 do without a .vc-config.toml (i.e. a Plain Old Repo), I'd like it to work if practical.

> AGENT

Good question. Practical and consistent design:

**Walk up from cwd to find `.vc-config.toml`. If none found, treat this repo as a single-repo workspace.**

In single-repo mode:
- Default `--scope=app` (only one repo, it's this one)
- `--scope=other` errors: `no other-repo configured (no .vc-config.toml found). Single-repo only — drop --scope or use --scope=app.`
- `--scope=both` same error
- All single-repo commands (`chid`, `desc`, `fix-desc`, `list`, `show`, single-repo `sync`/`push`/`finalize`) just work
- Workspace-only commands (e.g., `symlink` for `.claude`, dual-repo `chid -L`, `--squash --scope=both`) error out cleanly

Edge cases worth flagging in the design:
- POR with `.vc-config.toml` in a parent dir (workspace ancestor) → still found by walk-up; they'd be inside someone else's workspace. Maybe surprising; arguably an error worth emitting.
- A future `vc-x1 init --single` (or just running the existing `init` in a POR) could write a minimal config to upgrade to workspace mode if the user wants `.claude` later.

Scope question for the chores fold-in: 0.37.8's `## Confirm scope semantic + squash composition (0.37.8)` entry is already 3 paragraphs. Adding **(a)** cwd-independence + naming gotcha + counterpart-rename suggestion AND **(b)** POR handling would push it to 5+ paragraphs — getting unwieldy.

Two options:

1. **Fold both into 0.37.8 chores entry**, retitled to something broader (e.g. `## Scope design refinements (0.37.8)`).
2. **Split into two 0.37.x commits**: 0.37.8 stays as-is (scope semantic + composition + cwd-independence — those are tightly related); 0.37.9 captures POR handling separately as its own design subsection.

Lean: option 2 — POR handling is its own decision point (single-repo defaults, error messages, init-upgrade path) and deserves its own focused subsection rather than being a tail-end paragraph. Which do you prefer?

> DEVELOPER

First off my bad when I said path = "." as you said it's "/"!
Edge cases, when we start vc-x1 we should first consult ".vc-config.toml" the information is valid and thus vc-x1 always "knows" the right answer for the app and other scopes. POC occure two versions no .vc-config.toml or or there is but here is only `path` and no `other-repo`. thoughts?

> AGENT

No worries on the typo, easy to track.

POR thoughts:

**Both variants → single-repo mode, but distinguishable in error messaging.**

| Variant | What it looks like | scope behavior | Error msg on `--scope=other/both` |
|---|---|---|---|
| V1 (pure POR) | no `.vc-config.toml` | implicit `--scope=app`; this repo only | "not in a vc-x1 workspace (no `.vc-config.toml`). Single-repo mode — drop `--scope` or use `--scope=app`." |
| V2 (partial config) | `.vc-config.toml` with `path` only | implicit `--scope=app`; this repo only | "no other-repo configured. Add `other-repo = …` to `.vc-config.toml` to enable dual-repo operations." |

**Why `path` alone (V2) is still useful** — it gives the repo a stable workspace identity (even without a companion). Useful for ochid trailer formatting (`ochid: /<path-prefix>/<changeID>`), and for `vc-x1 chid` to render workspace-relative paths. So V2 isn't just "broken V3" — it's a valid "single-repo workspace, possibly with a companion later" state.

**Upgrade paths:**
- V1 → V2 → V3 via incremental config edits (`vc-x1 init` could automate, future).
- V3 (full workspace) downgrades aren't a concern — config edits are user-initiated.

**Edge cases worth catching:**
- V2 with `path != "/"` (e.g., `path = "/.claude"` but no `other-repo`) — the repo identifies itself as "the other side" but its companion is missing. Probably error: "config identifies this as `/.claude` but no `other-repo` to point at the app side; companion is missing."
- V3 with `other-repo` pointing somewhere that doesn't exist → loud error (workspace state corrupted), not POR fallback.

**On the chores fold-in:** with the cwd-independence + naming gotcha + POR (two variants) all on the table for capture, the original 0.37.8 entry is too narrow a title. Suggest **retitle to `## Scope design refinements (0.37.8)`** and bundle everything into one cohesive entry organized by bold-paragraph subtopics:

1. Scope semantic invariant (already drafted)
2. `--other` is project config, not per-command flag (already drafted)
3. Cwd independence + counterpart-rename suggestion (new, this turn)
4. POR handling: V1 vs V2 (new, this turn)
5. `vc-x1 push --squash` composition (already drafted)
6. Dogfood validation (already drafted)

Long but cohesive — all about scope/dispatcher design. Future readers find "everything about scope" in one place. OK to retitle and fold, or split as 0.37.9?

> DEVELOPER

Currently all repo's we've created that have .vc-config.toml are dual with one exception, vc-template-x1 it's prupose is to have the "typical" non-hidden files. I also have vc-template-x1.claude but we don't use that in "init", IIRC, and actually we should and it should have LICENSE-* or actually better we should add a .claude/ directory which that info so it's a single directory.

The V1, V2, V3 nomenclature is confusing I think maybe you're suggesting mult-step?

> AGENT

Fair — V1/V2/V3 read as a migration sequence; I meant a typology of states. Plain names are clearer:

- **POR** — no `.vc-config.toml`. Pure git/jj repo, vc-x1 doesn't know it exists.
- **Single-repo workspace** — `.vc-config.toml` with `path` only (no `other-repo`). vc-x1-aware, no companion. **Example: `vc-template-x1`.**
- **Dual-repo workspace** — `.vc-config.toml` with `path` + `other-repo`. Full setup. Example: `vc-x1` itself.

| State | Scope behavior | Error on `--scope=other/both` |
|---|---|---|
| POR | implicit `--scope=app` | "not in a vc-x1 workspace (no `.vc-config.toml`)..." |
| single-repo workspace | implicit `--scope=app` | "no other-repo configured. Add `other-repo = …` to `.vc-config.toml`..." |
| dual-repo workspace | `--scope=app/other/both` all valid | n/a |

Same three behaviors I described before, just descriptive names. Good catch — these read like states, not steps.

On `vc-template-x1` / `vc-template-x1.claude`:

- That `vc-template-x1` is a real "single-repo workspace" example is good design data — confirms this state isn't just hypothetical.
- The `.claude` subdir restructure (one template repo containing both sides) sounds clean, but it's a separate scope: requires changes to `init`, the template repo layout, and possibly `clone`. Suggest **add as new todo item** rather than fold into 0.37.8.

  3. Cwd independence + counterpart-rename suggestion (config field `other-repo` vs CLI `--scope=other` confusion; suggest renaming the config field to `counterpart` or `peer`).
  4. POR handling: three workspace states (POR / single-repo workspace / dual-repo workspace) with example `vc-template-x1` for the single-repo case, plus edge cases.

> AGENT

**Plan for #1 (Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection):**

Per chores-06 #63 design, this touches 6 command sites + 1 shared helper:
- New: `common::verify_tracking(repo, bookmark, remote)` helper using `jj bookmark list --tracked -T <template>`.
- `init`, `test-fixture`: post-condition assertions (likely already correct, just verify).
- `clone`: needs verification — may need explicit `jj bookmark track` after `jj git init --colocate` (open question per chores).
- `sync`, `push`, `finalize`: preflight detect + error with the remediation command (`jj bookmark track <b> --remote=<r> -R <repo>`).

**Version: 0.38.0** (minor bump — this rolls over from the 0.37.x dogfood cycle to a new feature area: cross-command tracking verification).

**Approach: multi-step (recommended), 3 dev commits + 1 release.**

| Step | Scope | Why this slicing |
|---|---|---|
| `0.38.0-0` | Shared `verify_tracking` helper + unit tests | Foundational, no behavior change; ships in isolation, easy review |
| `0.38.0-1` | Wire into setup commands: `init` (assertion), `clone` (verify + real fix if needed), `test-fixture` (assertion) | Setup commands all share "perform-then-verify" pattern; `clone` is the open-question one — bundling with init/test-fixture gives a cohesive checkpoint |
| `0.38.0-2` | Wire into preflight commands: `sync`, `push`, `finalize` | Preflight commands share "detect-then-error-out" pattern |
| `0.38.0` | Release commit — notes update, README touch-ups, any doc adjustments | Standard done-marker |

Single-step alternative would be ~200 LoC across 7 sites in one commit — workable but harder to review and noisier history.

OK to proceed with multi-step 0.38.0, starting with `0.38.0-0` (shared helper + tests)?

158	/// Scan `jj bookmark list -a <name>` output for non-tracking remote refs.
159	///
160	/// Tracked remotes appear indented (`  @origin: ...`), non-tracked remotes
161	/// appear at column 0 as `<bookmark>@<remote>: ...`. Returns the non-tracking
162	/// remote name if any is found.
163	fn find_non_tracking_remote(list_output: &str, bookmark: &str) -> Option<String> {
164	    let prefix = format!("{bookmark}@");
165	    for line in list_output.lines() {
166	        // Non-tracking remote ref is at column 0 and starts with "<bookmark>@"
167	        if line.starts_with(&prefix)
168	            && let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix(&prefix)
169	            && let Some((remote, _)) = rest.split_once(':')
170	        {
171	            return Some(remote.to_string());
172	        }
173	    }
174	    None
175	}
176	
177	/// Validate inputs synchronously before detaching.
178	///
179	/// Catches the common failure modes up front so the parent can exit
180	/// with a visible non-zero status — rather than discovering them in
181	/// the detached child where errors only reach the log file.
182	fn preflight(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
183	    debug!("preflight: checking opts");
184	    let repo_str = opts.repo.to_string_lossy();
185	    let cwd = std::path::Path::new(".");
186	
187	    // Verify squash revsets resolve to something.
188	    if let Some(ref sq) = opts.squash {
189	        jj_rev_exists(&repo_str, cwd, &sq.source)
190	            .map_err(|e| format!("squash source '{}' does not resolve: {e}", sq.source))?;
191	        jj_rev_exists(&repo_str, cwd, &sq.target)
192	            .map_err(|e| format!("squash target '{}' does not resolve: {e}", sq.target))?;
193	    }
194	
195	    // Refuse to operate on a repo with conflicts.
196	    let conflicts = run(
197	        "jj",
198	        &[
199	            "log",
200	            "-r",
201	            "conflicts()",
202	            "--no-graph",
203	            "-T",
204	            "\"x\"",
205	            "-R",
206	            &repo_str,
207	        ],
208	        cwd,
209	    )?;
210	    if !conflicts.is_empty() {
211	        return Err(format!("repo '{repo_str}' has conflicts — resolve before finalize").into());

213	
214	    // Bookmark: existence, tracking, forward-only move, push-target description.

218	            return Err(format!("bookmark '{bookmark}' does not exist").into());

```
160	/// Run a shell command. Returns stdout on success.
161	///
162	/// Logs the command at `debug!` and the process streams as follows:
163	/// - **stderr at `debug!`** on success — jj's chatter (`Moved 1 bookmarks
164	///   to …`, `Rebased N commits`, `Nothing changed.`) is hidden by default
165	///   and surfaced with `-v`. The debug formatter indents each line two
166	///   spaces so subprocess output sits visually under the caller's own
167	///   `info!` line in `-v` mode.
168	/// - **stdout at `debug!`** — callers usually consume stdout as data
169	///   (bookmark lists, commit IDs, etc.), and `info!` would flood the user
170	///   with machine-readable output they didn't ask for.
171	/// - **Failures propagate as `Err`** carrying the stderr; the caller's
172	///   error handler (`main::run_command`) surfaces it at `error!`.
173	pub fn run(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
174	    let args_str = args.join(" ");
175	    debug!("$ {cmd} {args_str}");
176	    trace!("cwd: {}", cwd.display());
177	    let output = std::process::Command::new(cmd)
178	        .args(args)
179	        .current_dir(cwd)
180	        .output()
181	        .map_err(|e| format!("failed to run {cmd}: {e}"))?;
182	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();
183	    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string();
184	    if !stdout.is_empty() {
185	        debug!("{stdout}");
186	    }
187	    if !output.status.success() {
188	        return Err(format!("{cmd} {args_str} failed: {stderr}").into());
189	    }
190	    if !stderr.is_empty() {
191	        debug!("{stderr}");
192	    }
193	    Ok(stdout)
194	}
195	
196	/// Create a directory (and parents). Logs at debug level.
197	pub fn mkdir_p(path: &Path) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
198	    debug!("mkdir -p {}", path.display());
199	    std::fs::create_dir_all(path)?;
200	    Ok(())
201	}
202	
203	/// Write content to a file. Logs at debug level.
204	pub fn write_file(path: &Path, content: &str) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
205	    debug!("write {}", path.display());
206	    std::fs::write(path, content)?;
207	    Ok(())
208	}
209	
210	/// Display a prompt, read a line from stdin, and log both together.
211	/// Returns the trimmed response.
212	pub fn prompt(msg: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
213	    use std::io::Write;
214	    eprint!("{msg}");
215	    std::io::stderr().flush()?;
216	    let mut response = String::new();
217	    std::io::stdin().read_line(&mut response)?;
218	    let trimmed = response.trim().to_string();
219	    info!("{msg}{trimmed}");
